Graph structure, adaptive regression, sensor placement and\\r\\nmulti-sensor information are also established. The reviewed confirmed cases do not supply a matched comparison for observed n1\\r\\nat +15 minutes using Area C tank/pump/AMR blocks under controlled reading delays. This is an evidence gap in this reviewed\\r\\ncorpus, not proof of absence from all literature; the unresolved cases limit the claim. ChebNet already illustrates the residual at\\r\\nnode 1 (paper Fig.5); choosing n1 itself is not novel.\\r\\nProposed contribution: a specific, falsifiable extension\\r\\nCandidate contribution\\r\\nWe propose to investigate whether received tank, pump, peer-pressure and AMR information improves the observed n1\\r\\npressure forecast at +15 minutes under unequal reading ages. Define r(u) as the last n1 reading received by u; learn\\r\\nthe increment p_n1(u+15)-r(u) and return r(u)+predicted_increment. Persistence uses that same r(u). The intended\\r\\ncontribution is a reproducible sector-specific comparison of incremental feature value and robustness, not a new regression\\r\\narchitecture.\\r\\nCandidate features include past tank-level changes and a storage proxy q_C=mean(Q_PUMP_1)-A*Delta_L/Delta_t (m3/h),\\r\\nusing nominal A=pi*(16 m)^2/4 and backward 15/30-minute windows. Tsinghua already uses this balance for diagnosis. Align\\r\\nlevel and averaged pump data to the same latest common time available by u; carry that time and age explicitly. Incomplete/stale\\r\\nwindows are masked, never completed with future readings. Rounding, nominal geometry and unmetered demands limit the proxy;\\r\\nit is not measured sector consumption.\\r\\nWith only two peer pressure sensors, use k={1,2}. A candidate score exp(-d_j/d0)*exp(-lambda*a_j/15) ranks nominal pipe\\r\\ndistance and reading age; lambda={0,0.5,1,2}. For k=1 compare nearest, freshest and the combined rule; k=2 uses both peers. A\\r\\ndistance proxy is not hydraulic sensitivity and must compete with simpler choices. No improvement or first-in-field novelty is\\r\\nasserted.\\r\\n\\r\\nPAGE 5 Mini-Project Milestone 2\\r\\nLearning Models for Pressure Forecasting\\r\\nDS675\\r\\nAndres Arango Martinez\\r\\nDraft v0.8 | 5/5\\r\\n5. Evaluation protocol and references\\r\\nCausal experiment and decision rules\\r\\nFit on January-September 2018, validate on October-December, purge origins whose +15 labels cross boundaries, and freeze\\r\\npolicies before the 2019 model assessment. Fit fills/scalers and selection on training only; do not randomly split windows. Compare\\r\\npersistence, Ridge, Random Forest and a static kNN temporal adaptation using the same target/information. For kNN, average\\r\\nthree received peer rows ending at u and label at u+15; this changes the SAM preprocessing lead and is not a reproduction of\\r\\nadaptive SAM-kNN. Predict increments around the last received n1 anchor, then score pressure levels.\\r\\nAblate own-history/calendar; add T1/PUMP_1; add peers; add three local AMRs/observed aggregates; test the wider 82-AMR\\r\\nblock separately. Distinguish the original channels, lags and derived feature counts. Simulate unequal auxiliary reading ages\\r\\n0/5/10/15 minutes with fixed seeds, independently of leak labels. Use last received readings, masks and ages; never shift a hidden\\r\\nfuture reading into X. Actual communication latency is not recorded. Primary delay experiments keep n1 current; a separate\\r\\nown-sensor-delay stress test uses the same delayed n1 anchor for every method. Select hyperparameters and delay schedules without\\r\\n2019 outcomes.\\r\\nReport MAE/RMSE in metres and skill=1-MAE_model/MAE_persistence, overall and by predeclared filling/draining regimes.\\r\\nCompare nearest/freshest/combined selection using the same model and peer budget. A claim of predictive value requires lower held-out error than both persistence and the same learner without the\\r\\nextra block; report failure if this is not achieved. Leak truth is evaluation metadata only, and a peer budget is not installed-sensor\\r\\ncost.\\r\\nRegression, kNN, feature engineering, regularization and ensemble comparisons connect to the course. Graph convolutions,\\r\\nalgorithmic transfer, adaptive memories and attention are techniques beyond that baseline. Own EDA motivates hypotheses but\\r\\ndoes not establish causal effects, novelty or 2019 skill. PAGE 1 Mini-Project Milestone 2
Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting
DS675
Andres Arango Martinez
Draft v0.9 | 1/5
1. Dataset and proposed learning task
Draft status. Working draft v0.9; not submitted. Literature/code were inspected; published training was not reproduced.
The proposed task is observed n1 pressure at +15 minutes. Own analysis below uses January-September 2018, not final
model results.
BattLeDIM is a simulated water-network benchmark inspired by Cyprus, originally designed for leak detection and localization
[1,2]. The official Zenodo archive supplies the data and nominal EPANET network; Kaggle is a secondary copy. Each of 2018 and
2019 contains 105,120 five-minute timestamps (210,240 total), with 119 measured channels: 33 pressures, 3 flows, 1 tank level
and 82 automatic-meter-reading (AMR) demands. The network has 782 junctions, 2 reservoirs, 1 tank, 905 pipes, 1 pump and 3
pressure-reducing valves.
Selected target, features and availability
Tank T1 supplies Area C, which contains 92 junctions and three pressure sensors: n1, n4 and n31. PUMP_1 fills T1 from the main
network; its flow is not the unmeasured sector-outlet flow through p239. n1 is the farthest of these three measured pressure nodes
from T1 by nominal pipe distance (891 m); this motivates its selection without claiming it is the globally critical node.
Block
Candidate inputs known at forecast origin u
Own and peer pressures
n1 history; current/lagged n4 and n31 (m).
Storage and inflow
T1 level (m), backward changes and lags; PUMP_1 flow (m3/h), lags and transitions.
Consumption / calendar
82 AMR rates (L/h), including n1/n4/n31; observed aggregates and known hour/day.
The response is y(u)=p_n1(u+15 min), three samples ahead. There are 87 raw candidate channels before lags/calendar
(3+1+1+82); the core using only the three local AMRs has eight. A feature may use only readings received by u. Future pressure,
tank, pump and AMR readings are excluded from predictors.
Figure 1. Observed daily n1, T1 and PUMP_1 profiles, 273 complete training-period days; red = clock-time mean. Full-size plots, all three
pressure sensors and correlation matrices are retained in the EDA companion [15].
Own EDA: n1 spans 25.67-29.36 m. Across 78,621 aligned training-period pairs, Pearson r with n1(+15) is 0.992 for current n1,
0.923 for T1 and -0.037 for PUMP_1. Association does not establish incremental forecast value. Descriptive persistence gives
MAE 0.0518 m / RMSE 0.0685 m; this is not 2019 performance. The future column is used only as an EDA response [15].

PAGE 2 Mini-Project Milestone 2
Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting
DS675
Andres Arango Martinez
Draft v0.9 | 2/5
2. Search, screening and corpus summary
Search strategy and filtering
This is a targeted exploratory review, not a systematic or exhaustive bibliometric review. The retained inventory was frozen on 12
October 2026 and re-audited on 15 October. The search began with the official dataset, benchmark paper, committee publications
and 18 linked competition proposals. It then followed references, author repositories and related graph, regression, anomaly and
temporal-learning work.
Representative queries were "BattLeDIM" "pressure forecasting", "L-Town" "pressure" "forecasting" learning, "BattLeDIM" "15-
minute" forecasting and title/author searches for ChebNet, SAM-kNN and AIGNN. Official/author/institutional texts and pinned
code were prioritized; other networks were retained as explicit methodological context. Versions, papers and their code were
grouped into shared records [14].
Figure 2. Counts recomputed from all classified records [14]. Panels A and B use different units. Primary-purpose percentages sum to 100%
before rounding; temporal components in C can occur inside a leak-detection study. Repository snapshots contain selected files, not necessarily
runnable clones.
Reading the denominator. The 59 retained sources form 43 grouped records, including reviews and support tools; they are not
43 independent experiments. Four records (9.3%) confirm pressure prediction after the last input: ChebNet, SAM-kNN, AIGNN
and external hDS-RNN. Three concern L-Town or a modification (3/43=7.0%; 3/36=8.3% of L-Town-tagged records, which include
reviews). The external case is never counted as a L-Town result.
Tsinghua describes temporal pressure VAR but does not fix a verifiable physical lead. AGN describes future states but pressure as
its output remains unconfirmed. Neither is included in the four confirmed records. Records lacking a demonstrated future-pressure
target are not evidence that their methods are incapable of forecasting. Initial search-hit totals and complete rejection counts were
not retained, so a PRISMA flow or search-recall estimate cannot be reconstructed. A 15 October spot-check located additional
leads outside this frozen inventory; they are logged separately, not silently added to these percentages.

PAGE 3 Mini-Project Milestone 2
Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting
DS675
Andres Arango Martinez
Draft v0.9 | 3/5
3. Previous temporal learning solutions
The three confirmed L-Town-family temporal solutions are included because they establish a positive pressure lead and represent
distinct mechanisms: spectral graph filtering, adaptive local regression and transferred algorithmic graph features. ChebNet and
SAM-kNN additionally provide inspectable temporal code. hDS-RNN is included only as an external multi-step methodological
contrast.
Figure 3. Techniques and verified physical leads [3-7,12]. One row = one grouped work; variants/baselines are not additional studies. Dashed
+15 is our proposal. History length, graph iterations and alarm delay are not forecast horizons.
ChebNet: graph topology plus historical pressure [3,6]
Gardarsson et al. use nominal hydraulic pressures to train a reconstructor for the current field and a predictor for the next field.
Four Chebyshev convolutions use hidden orders [240,120,20], filters [120,60,30], SiLU, sigmoid output and Adam/weight decay.
Historical readings are channels; selected T=3 is history, not a +15 lead. The pinned loader ends X at row i-1 and labels y[i],
confirming +5 minutes (data_loader.py:285-290). Predictor/reconstructor differences become edge residuals, seven-day statistics
and filtered alarms. The reported 2019 outcome is 19 TP, 138 FP, 4 FN and EUR326,521; it is not a pressure MAE. Nominal
healthy targets differ from our future observed pressure. Graph weighting and spatial information already predate our proposal.
SAM-kNN: online virtual pressure sensors [4,7]
Jakob et al. combine short-term, long-term and combined kNN memories, selecting by tracked error and cleaning/compressing
memories. Ten custom three-month scenarios use modified Area A: 661 nodes, 766 links and 29 pressure sensors. A virtual sensor
excludes its own pressure. The code averages three rows and labels the fourth with time_win_len=4: +5 minutes after the latest
input, despite less explicit paper wording. Online kNN (k=5, window=500) and linear regression share that labeling. The script
initializes with 3,000 processed examples and detects before fitting each 200-example batch. All five leaks are detected, with FP
counts 48/20/3/20/17; TP/FN count events while FP counts alarms. These custom scenarios are not distributed and are not the
original complete 2019 mixture.
AIGNN: transfer algorithmic reasoning to pressure prediction [5]
Zhang and Fink pretrain a processor on Ford-Fulkerson trajectories for 1,000 random 16-node graphs and transfer it to pressure
encoders/decoders; ChebNetIN/EMB inject its features. Inputs x(t-T)...x(t-1) predict y(t): one five-minute step. Nominal
simulation supplies training labels, 2018 tunes diagnostic thresholds and 2019 evaluates detection. Preprint Table 3 reports relative
prediction errors (%) of 0.3774 +/- 0.1588 for ChebNet, 0.3249 +/- 0.0685 AIGNN, 0.2964 +/- 0.0874 IN and 0.2077 +/- 0.0074
EMB; these are not MAE in metres. No AIGNN author-code snapshot is retained. Sensor-relocation generalization is already prior
work.
External hDS-RNN: multi-step pressure forecasts [12]
Ma et al. use an LSTM encoder/decoder with hybrid spatial and temporal attention on another real network, with pressure
target P5, 30-minute sampling and 60 history readings. Four outputs span +30/+60/+90/+120 minutes. Differencing/seasonal
preparation and chronological splitting are described. Table 1 reports P5 MAE 0.0818 versus DS-RNN 0.0830 (paper scale not
established here as physical metres); not a L-Town result. Its ten-step extension figure concerns flow, not pressure. No author
repository was verified.

PAGE 4 Mini-Project Milestone 2
Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting
DS675
Andres Arango Martinez
Draft v0.9 | 4/5
4. Prior work, gap and proposed contribution
Prior Work includes current-state and incompletely resolved cases because they challenge specific novelty claims. They are selected
for target clarity, useful evidence or close overlap with our inputs, rather than high scores or publication date alone. The full
classified inventory remains accessible [14].
Selected source
Distinct contribution and why it matters here
LILA [8,9]
Current-condition pressure/flow regression, CUSUM and hydraulic localization; pinned code provides a
non-temporal boundary case. All 19 new 2019 leaks were identified; at 300 m over the leak lifespan, two
out-of-radius locations yield 17 TP, 2 FP, 0 FN. Four inherited leaks are excluded. This is not forecast accuracy.
GATRes [10]
Residual graph attention reconstructs masked current pressures in generated snapshots. A weekly
demand-pattern test gives MAE 0.9501 +/- 0.0086 m vs mGCN 1.5928 +/- 0.0050 m; the ranking changes under
another distribution. Useful spatial/missing-sensor reference, not a temporal performance comparator.
Tsinghua / VAR [13]
Normal pressure is modeled from history with exogenous series; exact lead, lag order and exogenous availability
are unspecified. Area C already combines pump flow, tank-level changes and AMRs for leak diagnosis, including a
30-minute balance to reduce level-data artifacts. Neither tank inputs nor this balance can be claimed as new.
AGN [11]
The publisher describes attention-augmented gated graph learning to forecast nodal demand from pressure
measurements. This is an adjacent target and an overlap warning. We have not verified a prospective pressure
output or its physical lead; it is excluded from confirmed pressure forecasting. No author-code snapshot was
validated.
What is established, and what remains to be tested
Pressure forecasting is already part of L-Town leakage pipelines. Graph structure, adaptive regression, sensor placement and
multi-sensor information are also established. The reviewed confirmed cases do not supply a matched comparison for observed n1
at +15 minutes using Area C tank/pump/AMR blocks under controlled reading delays. This is an evidence gap in this reviewed
corpus, not proof of absence from all literature; the unresolved cases limit the claim. ChebNet already illustrates the residual at
node 1 (paper Fig.5); choosing n1 itself is not novel.
Proposed contribution: a specific, falsifiable extension
Candidate contribution
We propose to investigate whether received tank, pump, peer-pressure and AMR information improves the observed n1
pressure forecast at +15 minutes under unequal reading ages. Define r(u) as the last n1 reading received by u; learn
the increment p_n1(u+15)-r(u) and return r(u)+predicted_increment. Persistence uses that same r(u). The intended
contribution is a reproducible sector-specific comparison of incremental feature value and robustness, not a new regression
architecture. Specifically, test whether a past-only storage proxy and explicit ages/masks improve upon the same learner
with raw histories under filling/draining and delayed auxiliary data. That matched predictive comparison is the proposed
extension beyond the reviewed diagnostic/current-state uses; its benefit and originality remain to be established.
Use complete backward windows of w=15/30 minutes, ending at the latest common level/flow time v available by u. Average pump
flow over that same window. The nominal tank area A follows its 16 m diameter [1]. Define the storage proxy (m3/h), with w/60
in hours:
e
QC(v; w) = QPUMP_1,[v−w,v] −A L(v) −L(v −w)
w/60
,
A = π(16 m)2/4.
Tsinghua already uses this balance for diagnosis [13]. Test raw histories alone against histories plus this proxy for a future-pressure
target. Carry v and its age u-v; mask incomplete windows. Rounding, nominal geometry and missing measurements limit accuracy;
this is not measured sector consumption.
Only two pressure peers exist. Compare k=1 and k=2; ranking matters only for k=1. A candidate score combines nominal pipe
distance d_j from n1 (d0 = median positive peer distance) with reading age a_j in minutes. Validate lambda in {0,0.5,1,2} against
nearest-only, freshest-only and fixed-peer choices. Distance is not verified hydraulic sensitivity:
Sj(u) = exp(−dj/d0) exp[−λ aj(u)/(15 min)].

PAGE 5 Mini-Project Milestone 2
Learning Models for Pressure Forecasting
DS675
Andres Arango Martinez
Draft v0.9 | 5/5
5. Evaluation protocol and references
Causal experiment and decision rules
Train on January-September 2018; validate on October-December; purge origins whose +15 labels cross a boundary. Fit fills/scalers
on training only; never randomly split windows. Freeze model/features/delays before final 2019 assessment. Because 2019 is a
public, previously inspected benchmark, this is not a pristine blind test. Compare persistence, Ridge, Random Forest and plain
kNN on identical target/available feature blocks and validation budgets. The kNN temporal baseline labels three received rows
ending at u with p(u+15); it is not a reproduction of published SAM-kNN. Reconstruct pressure levels around the last received n1
anchor.
Ablate own-history/calendar; add T1/PUMP_1; add peers; add three local AMRs/observed aggregates; test 82 AMRs separately.
Report raw/lag/derived dimensions. Tune histories {3,6,12}, proxy windows {15,30} minutes and selection only on 2018 validation.
Simulate reproducible unequal auxiliary ages {0,5,10,15} minutes independently of pressure/leak truth, with last-received data,
masks and ages. Keep n1 current in the primary experiment; a separate own-delay test gives all methods the same n1 anchor.
Latencies are simulated, not recorded.
Report MAE/RMSE (m), skill=1-MAE_model/MAE_persistence and matched errors by regime defined at origin from received
PUMP_1 flow and backward T1 change. Compare fixed peers, nearest, freshest and combined selection at equal k and learner;
include raw-only/proxy and age/mask ablations. Summarize paired losses with contiguous-week block bootstrap and block-length
sensitivity, not independent five-minute error bars. A positive result must beat persistence and the matched same-learner baseline
on the predeclared primary comparison; report null/negative results. Leak truth never enters X.
Regression, kNN, feature engineering, regularization and ensemble comparisons connect to the course. Graph convolutions,
algorithmic transfer, adaptive memories and attention are techniques beyond that baseline. Own EDA motivates hypotheses but
does not establish causal effects, novelty or 2019 skill. No learned model has been trained for this report.
Limits and references
The proposed contribution is a testable predictive extension within this reviewed corpus; no first-in-field or guaranteed-gain claim
is supported. Public-data exposure, unresolved leads, unavailable scenarios and nominal hydraulic uncertainty remain. If no
incremental value is found, report it honestly. Alternatives for a separately checked proposal include calibrated pressure intervals
or transfer to another measured node; neither is claimed novel here. Full evidence and EDA remain linked [14,15].
[1] Vrachimis et al. Official BattLeDIM dataset and nominal network.
[2] Vrachimis et al. (2022). BattLeDIM benchmark, tasks and scoring.
[3] Gardarsson, Boem and Toni (2022). Graph-based learning for leak detection.
[4] Jakob et al. (2022). SAM-kNN regressor for online learning in WDNs.
[5] Zhang and Fink (2024 preprint). Algorithm-Informed Graph Neural Networks.
[6] ChebNet code: pinned loader, predictor and reconstructor.
[7] SAM-kNN code: pinned preprocessing, models and experiments.

Graph structure, adaptive regression, sensor placement and\\r\\nmulti-sensor information are also established. The reviewed confirmed cases do not supply a matched comparison for observed n1\\r\\nat +15 minutes using Area C tank/pump/AMR blocks under controlled reading delays. This is an evidence gap in this reviewed\\r\\ncorpus, not proof of absence from all literature; the unresolved cases limit the claim. ChebNet already illustrates the residual at\\r\\nnode 1 (paper Fig.5); choosing n1 itself is not novel.\\r\\nProposed contribution: a specific, falsifiable extension\\r\\nCandidate contribution\\r\\nWe propose to investigate whether received tank, pump, peer-pressure and AMR information improves the observed n1\\r\\npressure forecast at +15 minutes under unequal reading ages. Define r(u) as the last n1 reading received by u; learn\\r\\nthe increment p_n1(u+15)-r(u) and return r(u)+predicted_increment. Persistence uses that same r(u). The intended\\r\\ncontribution is a reproducible sector-specific comparison of incremental feature value and robustness, not a new regression\\r\\narchitecture. Specifically, test whether a past-only storage proxy and explicit ages/masks improve upon the same learner\\r\\nwith raw histories under filling/draining and delayed auxiliary data. That matched predictive comparison is the proposed\\r\\nextension beyond the reviewed diagnostic/current-state uses; its benefit and originality remain to be established.\\r\\nUse complete backward windows of w=15/30 minutes, ending at the latest common level/flow time v available by u. Average pump\\r\\nflow over that same window. The nominal tank area A follows its 16 m diameter [1]. Define the storage proxy (m3/h), with w/60\\r\\nin hours:\\r\\ne\\r\\nQC(v; w) = QPUMP_1,[v−w,v] −A L(v) −L(v −w)\\r\\nw/60\\r\\n,\\r\\nA = π(16 m)2/4.\\r\\nTsinghua already uses this balance for diagnosis [13]. Test raw histories alone against histories plus this proxy for a future-pressure\\r\\ntarget. Carry v and its age u-v; mask incomplete windows. Rounding, nominal geometry and missing measurements limit accuracy;\\r\\nthis is not measured sector consumption.\\r\\nOnly two pressure peers exist. Compare k=1 and k=2; ranking matters only for k=1. A candidate score combines nominal pipe\\r\\ndistance d_j from n1 (d0 = median positive peer distance) with reading age a_j in minutes. Validate lambda in {0,0.5,1,2} against\\r\\nnearest-only, freshest-only and fixed-peer choices. Distance is not verified hydraulic sensitivity:\\r\\nSj(u) = exp(−dj/d0) exp[−λ aj(u)/(15 min)].\\r\\n\\r\\nPAGE 5 Mini-Project Milestone 2\\r\\nLearning Models for Pressure Forecasting\\r\\nDS675\\r\\nAndres Arango Martinez\\r\\nDraft v0.9 | 5/5\\r\\n5. Evaluation protocol and references\\r\\nCausal experiment and decision rules\\r\\nTrain on January-September 2018; validate on October-December; purge origins whose +15 labels cross a boundary. Fit fills/scalers\\r\\non training only; never randomly split windows. Freeze model/features/delays before final 2019 assessment. Because 2019 is a\\r\\npublic, previously inspected benchmark, this is not a pristine blind test. Compare persistence, Ridge, Random Forest and plain\\r\\nkNN on identical target/available feature blocks and validation budgets. The kNN temporal baseline labels three received rows\\r\\nending at u with p(u+15); it is not a reproduction of published SAM-kNN. Reconstruct pressure levels around the last received n1\\r\\nanchor.\\r\\nAblate own-history/calendar; add T1/PUMP_1; add peers; add three local AMRs/observed aggregates; test 82 AMRs separately.\\r\\nReport raw/lag/derived dimensions. Tune histories {3,6,12}, proxy windows {15,30} minutes and selection only on 2018 validation.\\r\\nSimulate reproducible unequal auxiliary ages {0,5,10,15} minutes independently of pressure/leak truth, with last-received data,\\r\\nmasks and ages. Keep n1 current in the primary experiment; a separate own-delay test gives all methods the same n1 anchor.\\r\\nLatencies are simulated, not recorded.\\r\\nReport MAE/RMSE (m), skill=1-MAE_model/MAE_persistence and matched errors by regime defined at origin from received\\r\\nPUMP_1 flow and backward T1 change. A positive result must beat persistence and the matched same-learner baseline\\r\\non the predeclared primary comparison; report null/negative results. Leak truth never enters X.\\r\\nRegression, kNN, feature engineering, regularization and ensemble comparisons connect to the course. ## Nivel T1, caudal PUMP_1 y matriz de correlaciones · v0.6
# Milestone 2 · objetivo y aporte candidato

**Estado: propuesta en discusión.** No hay entrenamiento, comparación de modelos propios ni novedad científica confirmada.

## Objetivo que conservamos

Pronosticar la presión registrada en **n1 del Área C** a +15 minutos, con historia n1/n4/n31, nivel T1, caudal PUMP_1, AMR y calendario disponibles al emitir el pronóstico.

Ejemplo: a las 10:00, utilizar información conocida hasta las 10:00 para emitir el pronóstico de la lectura de las 10:15. Los registros de las 10:05, 10:10 y 10:15 no pueden entrar como predictores.

El dataset registra cada cinco minutos y la presión corresponde a un promedio del intervalo precedente. La lectura etiquetada 10:15 representa ese registro agregado; no es una presión instantánea inventada. Tres pasos es el horizonte elegido, no un retardo hidráulico identificado.

## Qué ya tiene antecedentes

- Garðarsson y AIGNN combinan reconstrucción actual y predicción del siguiente paso para diagnóstico.
- SAM-kNN ya realiza predicción temporal y adaptación en escenarios propios.
- LILA estima referencias entre presiones y caudales para producir residuos de fugas.
- Topología, sensores escasos, interpretabilidad, ruido y recalibración aparecen en otros trabajos de L-Town.

Por ello, cambiar +5 a +15 minutos, escoger Random Forest, modelar incrementos o usar topología no demuestra novedad por sí solo. Evitar información futura es un requisito de validez.

## Pregunta de extensión recomendada

¿Qué información conviene utilizar para pronosticar a +15 minutos cuando algunas mediciones hidráulicamente relacionadas están más atrasadas que otras?

Se propone desarrollar una **regla de selección o uso de mediciones que considere relación hidráulica y antigüedad de la lectura**, bajo un presupuesto explícito de sensores. La regla todavía debe definirse matemáticamente y confrontarse con literatura específica; no se presenta como un algoritmo terminado.

Por ejemplo, al emitir el pronóstico a las 10:00, un sensor relacionado podría tener última lectura a las 09:50 y otro menos relacionado, a las 10:00. Comparar ambas fuentes permite investigar el valor de la información y su frescura.

## Comparaciones necesarias

| Comparación | Qué permite comprobar |
|---|---|
| Persistencia con la última lectura realmente disponible | Ganancia respecto a inercia |
| Referencia estacional causal y regresión regularizada/VAR | Valor más allá de baselines temporales sencillas |
| Selección por correlación | Referencia estadística de igual presupuesto |
| Selección por relación hidráulica | Valor de estructura sin incorporar antigüedad |
| Regla propuesta | Valor incremental de considerar antigüedad |
| Todas las entradas | Costo y utilidad de observar más variables |

Para aislar el efecto de selección, utilizar la misma familia de modelo, capacidad, rezagos y presupuesto de tuning. Comparar subconjuntos de igual tamaño; reportar por separado sensores, columnas y rezagos. El acceso a la presión del propio sensor objetivo debe ser igual entre alternativas.

## Protocolo por fijar antes de entrenar

1. n1 es el objetivo de trabajo seleccionado; fijar las demás decisiones con 2018 y reservar 2019 para evaluación final.
2. Construir la etiqueta a +3 pasos verificando timestamps y límites de split. Ninguna etiqueta de train puede depender del período reservado.
3. Ajustar escalado, selección y otros parámetros solo con los datos permitidos en cada fold temporal.
4. Empezar con datos oficiales sin perturbaciones y baselines fuertes.
5. En una extensión, simular edades de 0/5/10/15 minutos en canales auxiliares. El dataset no contiene latencias reales documentadas.
6. Usar únicamente imputación causal y registrar la antigüedad. Mantener etiquetas originales observadas.
7. Evaluar MAE/RMSE en metros y skill frente a persistencia por mes/sensor y regímenes predefinidos. Las etiquetas de fugas se usan para evaluación, fuera de las entradas.

Si se retrasa la lectura del propio sensor objetivo, persistencia debe usar también su última lectura recibida, no un valor más reciente oculto al modelo. Para aprendizaje adaptativo, una etiqueta de un pronóstico emitido en t llega como mínimo en t+15.

## Alternativas si el aporte pierde fuerza

| Alternativa | Espacio concreto | Antecedentes que reconocer |
|---|---|---|
| Intervalos de presión futura | Cobertura y amplitud por régimen con calibración temporal | Incertidumbre y benchmarking ya tienen literatura; no asumir garantía i.i.d. |
| Adaptación con etiquetas demoradas | Comparar congelado, periódico y adaptativo bajo el mismo acceso | SAM-kNN y recalibración SICAMS |
| Alarma desde residuos | Reducir falsas alarmas/demora a igual recall por evento | Garðarsson, AIGNN y SICAMS; cambia el objetivo del proyecto |

## Decisiones pendientes

Completar la semántica del target y horizontes de AGN, buscar antecedentes específicos de selección con lecturas atrasadas, validar presupuesto/regla para el Área C. Después será posible decidir qué aporte podemos defender. Un resultado negativo también es informativo, pero no reemplaza diseñar una extensión justificada.

## Primera regla implementable · borrador v0.4

Como punto de partida se propone usar la distancia nominal por caminos de tuberías, normalizada por la mediana positiva de distancias, como proxy explícito de relación hidráulica. El score de un sensor combina `exp(-d_j/d0)` y `exp(-lambda * edad_j / 15 minutos)`. Se eligen los k pares conectados disponibles con mayor score; si no hay k elegibles, se usan los disponibles. Distancia no equivale a sensibilidad hidráulica: se debe validar esa aproximación o sustituirla por una respuesta nominal de presión.

Con n1 como target y n4/n31 como únicos pares del sector, los valores candidatos son k=1/2 y lambda=0/0,5/1/2, ajustados con validación de 2018. Comparar distancia sola y correlación de entrenamiento a igual presupuesto; todos los pares constituyen una referencia de mayor presupuesto. El diseño debe conservar columnas por sensor, máscaras y edades. Se simularán edades **desiguales** independientes de presión y etiquetas: edades idénticas en todos los sensores no cambian el ranking. n1 ya está seleccionado como objetivo de trabajo; la validación del aporte sigue pendiente.

## Variables y gráficas actuales · v0.5

El conjunto candidato comprende 87 señales medidas: 3 presiones, 1 nivel, 1 caudal y 82 AMR, antes de rezagos/calendario. PUMP_1 llena T1 y no es caudal de salida medido del sector. Comparar historia propia/calendario, adición de tanque/bomba, presiones vecinas y AMR. [Ver perfiles diarios, medias y código](#/miniproject/milestone-2/area-c).

---
name: "pdf"
description: "Read, create, inspect, render, and verify PDF files where visual layout matters, including fillable AcroForms. Use Poppler rendering plus Python tools such as reportlab, pdfplumber, and pypdf for generation and extraction."
---

# PDF Skill

## When To Use

- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Fill and validate interactive PDF forms.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Tools + Contract Requirements

Immediately before the first create/edit authoring command, run `mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs` successfully exactly once using the command below. Do not run it for read-only work. For edits, replace `create` with `edit`; adjust the expected count and output format to match the requested outputs.



```bash
node container_tools/mark_artifact_operation_started.mjs --operation-kind create --expected-output-count 1 --output-format pdf
```

## Workflow

1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` from the bundled runtime or system Poppler when available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` or `pypdf` for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on text extraction for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Fill And Validate AcroForms

Visual review alone is not a correctness check for a fillable PDF. A page `/Widget` annotation can render a value from its appearance stream while the canonical `/AcroForm/Fields` tree is missing or contains a stale value.

1. Keep the result interactive by default; set `flatten=True` only when the user explicitly requests a completed, static form. Preserve the source PDF, and do not flatten a signed PDF without an explicit workflow decision.
2. Inspect both representations before filling: enumerate fields from `reader.get_fields()` and `/Widget` annotations from every page's `/Annots`, following `/Parent` and `/Kids`. If a widget and a canonical field have the same name but are distinct objects with no `/Parent` relationship, do not call `reattach_fields()` blindly: it can create a second top-level field with the same name. Report the ambiguity or produce a static result.
3. Recover genuinely orphaned widgets, fill all pages, and write the result with `pypdf`:

```python
from pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter
from pypdf.generic import NameObject

reader = PdfReader(input_pdf)
writer = PdfWriter()
writer.clone_document_from_reader(reader)

# Restores widgets that are missing from /AcroForm/Fields.
writer.reattach_fields()
fields = writer.get_fields() or {}
missing = set(expected_values) - set(fields)
if missing:
    raise ValueError(f"Form fields not found after repair: {sorted(missing)}")

values_to_write = dict(expected_values)
if flatten:
    # Paint every existing value before removing every widget.
    values_to_write = {
        name: field.get("/V", "/Off" if field.get("/FT") == "/Btn" else "")
        for name, field in fields.items()
    }
    values_to_write.update(expected_values)

writer.update_page_form_field_values(
    None, values_to_write, auto_regenerate=False, flatten=flatten
)

if flatten:
    # pypdf's flatten=True paints appearances but does not remove widgets.
    writer.remove_annotations(subtypes="/Widget")
    writer.root_object.pop(NameObject("/AcroForm"), None)

with open(output_pdf, "wb") as stream:
    writer.write(stream)
```

4. Reopen the written PDF before delivery. For an interactive result, require every expected field to be present in `get_fields()` with the expected `/V`, enumerate page widgets again, and confirm their effective `/V` (the widget value or inherited `/Parent` value) agrees. Confirm each updated widget has a non-empty `/AP` `/N` appearance and render the final pages to catch stale or clipped appearances. Do not rely on `/NeedAppearances` or a successful PNG render as proof that logical field data was updated.
5. For a flattened result, require zero `/Widget` annotations and no remaining `/AcroForm` field tree after reopening, then render the final pages. Keep an editable copy when the user may need to revise the form.

## Temp And Output Conventions

- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete them when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies

Prefer the Codex bundled workspace/runtime dependencies when available. The primary runtime is expected to include:

- Python packages: `reportlab`, `pdfplumber`, `pypdf`
- Rendering tools: `pdftoppm` and `pdfinfo` from Poppler

If a dependency is missing, install only what is needed.

Python packages:

```bash
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

If `uv` is unavailable:

```bash
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```

System tools for rendering:

```bash
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation is not possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment

No required environment variables.

## Rendering Command

```bash
pdftoppm -png "$INPUT_PDF" "$OUTPUT_PREFIX"
```

## Quality Expectations

- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final Checks

- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers, footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

## Final response

### Final Response Citations

- **Create/edit:** for created or edited PDFs, cite each final deliverable exactly once with `purpose="output"`, in its own paragraph. Briefly describe the result without repeating its filename, path, or link. Never put output directives inside prose or list items.
- **Q&A and summaries:** Every file whose contents you summarize or use to support an answer MUST receive a `:codex-file-citation{...}` with `purpose="source"` in the final response. Use the source citation in place of the filename, wherever the filename would appear. Do not write the filename separately and append its citation at the end. Files merely inspected but not used in the answer need no citation. Do not emit output citations for unchanged files or read-only answers.

- Emit `:codex-file-citation{...}` directly, without backticks, code fences, or a Markdown-link wrapper. Use verified targets only. No need to cite or reference intermediate work.

For file citations, set `path` to the verified absolute file path on the task host. Omit `mode`.

Some illustrative examples, real responses use verified targets outside code fences.

```text

```

```text
